Istio's control plane provides an abstraction\\n43: layer over the underlying cluster management platform, such as Kubernetes.\\n44: \\n45: Istio is composed of these components:\\n46: \\n47: - **Envoy** - Sidecar proxies per microservice to handle ingress/egress traffic\\n48:    between services in the cluster and from a service to external\\n49:    services. The proxies form a _secure microservice mesh_ providing a rich\\n50:    set of functions like discovery, rich layer-7 routing, circuit breakers,\\n51:    policy enforcement and telemetry recording/reporting\\n52:    functions.\\n53: \\n54:   > Note: The service mesh is not an overlay network. It\\n55:   > simplifies and enhances how microservices in an application talk to each\\n56:   > other over the network provided by the underlying platform.\\n57: \\n58: - **Istiod** - The Istio control plane. It consists of the following sub-components:\\n59: \\n60:     - **Pilot** - Responsible for configuring the proxies at runtime.\\n61: \\n62:     - **Citadel** - Responsible for certificate issuance and rotation.\\n63: \\n64:     - **Galley** - Responsible for validating, ingesting, aggregating, transforming and distributing config within Istio.\\n65: \\n66: - **Operator** - The component provides user friendly options to operate the Istio service mesh.\\n67: \\n68: ## Repositories\\n69: \\n70: The Istio project is divided across a few GitHub repositories:\\n71: \\n72: - [istio/api](https://github.com/istio/api). So we can test\\n24: # by trying to run a trivial program and ensuring it actually ran. If not, emit our warning.\\n25: # Note: we cannot do anything like $(shell docker version) to check, since that would also fail.\\n26: CAN_RUN := $(shell echo \\\"can I run echo\\\")\\n27: ifeq ($(CAN_RUN),)\\n28: $(error $(warning))\\n29: endif\\n30: \\n31: #-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n32: # Global Variables\\n33: #-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n34: ISTIO_GO := $(shell dirname $(realpath $(lastword $(MAKEFILE_LIST))))\\n35: export ISTIO_GO\\n36: SHELL := /bin/bash -o pipefail\\n37: \\n38: # Version can be defined:\\n39: # (1) in a $VERSION shell variable, which takes precedence; or\\n40: # (2) in the VERSION file, in which we will append \\\"-dev\\\" to it\\n41: ifeq ($(VERSION),)\\n42: VERSION_FROM_FILE := $(shell cat VERSION)\\n43: ifeq ($(VERSION_FROM_FILE),)\\n44: $(error VERSION not detected. Make sure it's stored in the VERSION file or defined in VERSION variable)\\n45: endif\\n46: VERSION := $(VERSION_FROM_FILE)-dev\\n47: endif\\n48: \\n49: export VERSION\\n50: \\n51: # Base version of Istio image to use\\n52: BASE_VERSION ?= 1.28-2026-01-13T19-03-16\\n53: ISTIO_BASE_REGISTRY ?= gcr.io/istio-release\\n54: \\n55: export GO111MODULE ?= on\\n56: export GOPROXY ?= https://proxy.golang.org\\n57: export GOSUMDB ?= sum.golang.org\\n58: \\n59: # {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01V1YRhA44ZHczFS3EfuCn5g","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_015EdzgpuKYeiiMtgLozyCZj","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Istio Control Plane Developer Onboarding Guide\n\nThis guide is designed to help new contributors understand how to build, develop, and contribute to the Istio control plane. The control plane (istiod) is the core component that manages service discovery, configuration, and security in the Istio service mesh.\n\n---\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n1. [Build Prerequisites](#build-prerequisites)\n2. [Architecture Overview](#architecture-overview)\n3. [First Contribution Workflow](#first-contribution-workflow)\n\n---\n\n## Build Prerequisites\n\n### System Requirements\n\n**Go Version:** Go 1.24.0 or later\n\nThe project uses Go modules (`GO111MODULE=on`). You can verify your Go installation:\n\n```bash\ngo version\n```\n\n### Clone the Repository\n\nClone the repository with all submodules:\n\n```bash\ngit clone --recursive https://github.com/istio/istio.git\ncd istio\n```\n\nIf you've already cloned without submodules:\n\n```bash\ngit submodule update --init --recursive\n```\n\n### Build Options\n\nIstio supports two build modes:\n\n#### Option 1: Local Build (Recommended for Development)\n\nThis uses your local Go toolchain:\n\n```bash\nexport BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=0\nmake build\n```\n\n#### Option 2: Container Build\n\nUses Docker to build in a consistent environment (only requires `make` and `docker`):\n\n```bash\nexport BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=1\nmake build\n```\n\nIf you don't set `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER`, it defaults to `0` (local build).\n\n### Building Core Binaries\n\nThe main control plane components are built from the following targets:\n\n**Build all components:**\n```bash\nmake build\n```\n\n**Build specific binaries:**\n```bash\nmake pilot-discovery    # Main Pilot/istiod discovery server\nmake pilot-agent        # Sidecar proxy agent\nmake istioctl           # CLI tool for managing Istio\n```\n\nThese binaries are placed in `$(TARGET_OUT)` (default: `out/release/`).\n\n**Build for Linux containers:**\n```bash\nmake build-linux\n```\n\nThis is required before building Docker images and outputs to `out/linux_release/`.\n\n### Initialize Build Environment\n\nBefore your first build, initialize the build environment to download Envoy and other dependencies:\n\n```bash\nmake init\n```\n\nThis step:\n- Downloads the Envoy binary (matching the SHA defined in `pilot/docker/Dockerfile.base`)\n- Prepares dependencies declared in `istio.deps`\n\nThe initialization output is placed in `$(TARGET_OUT)/istio_is_init`.\n\n### Build Configuration\n\nKey environment variables and Makefile variables:\n\n| Variable | Purpose | Example |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER` | Use Docker for builds | `0` (local) or `1` (container) |\n| `TARGET_OUT` | Output directory for binaries | `out/release/` |\n| `ISTIO_BIN` | Directory for test binaries | `$(GOPATH)/bin` |\n| `DEBUG` | Enable debug symbols | `0` (default), `1` (enable) |\n| `GOPROXY` | Go module proxy | `https://proxy.golang.org` |\n\nExample: Build with debug symbols:\n```bash\nDEBUG=1 make build\n```\n\n### Verify Your Build\n\nAfter building, verify the binaries were created:\n\n```bash\nls -l out/release/pilot-discovery\nls -l out/release/pilot-agent\nls -l out/release/istioctl\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n### Control Plane Components\n\nThe Istio control plane (istiod) is a unified component that manages three primary functions:\n\n#### 1. **Pilot** - Configuration and Discovery\n- **Responsibility:** Manages service discovery and pushes dynamic configuration to Envoy proxies\n- **Key Packages:** `pilot/pkg/xds/`, `pilot/pkg/model/`, `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/`\n- **Entry Point:** `pilot/cmd/pilot-discovery/main.go` → `app.NewRootCommand()`\n\nPilot converts Kubernetes Service and Istio configuration objects into Envoy configuration. It serves as the xDS server that proxies connect to for runtime updates.\n\n**Example Data Flow:**\n1. Administrator creates a `VirtualService` resource in Kubernetes\n2. Pilot watches Kubernetes API and detects the change\n3. Pilot's configuration controller processes the `VirtualService`\n4. Pilot's model generates updated Envoy configuration (via `pilot/pkg/networking/`)\n5. Pilot pushes updates to connected proxies via xDS\n\n#### 2. **Citadel** - Certificate Management\n- **Responsibility:** Issues and rotates certificates for mTLS between services\n- **Key Packages:** `security/pkg/pki/`, `security/pkg/server/`\n\nCitadel manages the service mesh PKI (Public Key Infrastructure) by:\n- Issuing short-lived certificates to workloads\n- Managing certificate rotation\n- Implementing secret discovery service (SDS) for distributing certificates to proxies\n\n#### 3. **Galley** - Configuration Validation and Distribution (Now Integrated into Pilot)\n- **Responsibility:** Validates Istio configuration before it's applied\n- **Key Packages:** `pilot/pkg/config/`\n\nGalley validates configuration and ensures consistency across the mesh.\n\n### The xDS Protocol\n\nThe core communication mechanism between Pilot and Envoy proxies is **xDS** (extensible Discovery Service), a set of APIs defined by Envoy:\n\n| Protocol | Purpose | Package |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| **ADS** | Aggregated Discovery Service - main gRPC stream | `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go` |\n| **CDS** | Cluster Discovery Service - defines upstream clusters | `pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go` |\n| **RDS** | Route Discovery Service - defines routing rules | `pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go` |\n| **LDS** | Listener Discovery Service - defines listeners | `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go` |\n| **EDS** | Endpoint Discovery Service - defines service endpoints | `pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go` |\n| **SDS** | Secret Discovery Service - distributes certificates | `pilot/pkg/xds/sds.go` |\n| **ECDS** | Extension Config Discovery Service | `pilot/pkg/xds/ecds.go` |\n\n**xDS Data Flow Example:**\n\n```\n1. Envoy proxy connects to Pilot via gRPC (ADS)\n   └─> Connection handled by DiscoveryStream in pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go\n\n2. Proxy sends discovery request:\n   ├─> \"Give me my listeners\" (LDS)\n   ├─> \"Give me my routes\" (RDS)\n   ├─> \"Give me my clusters\" (CDS)\n   └─> \"Give me my endpoints\" (EDS)\n\n3. Pilot generates configuration:\n   ├─> LDS Generator creates listener config (pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go:119)\n   ├─> RDS Generator creates route config (pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go)\n   ├─> CDS Generator creates cluster config (pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go)\n   └─> EDS Generator creates endpoints (pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go)\n\n4. Pilot pushes response back to proxy\n   └─> Proxy applies configuration to its traffic handling\n\n5. When config changes (e.g., new Service):\n   ├─> Kubernetes event triggers Pilot's watchers\n   ├─> Pilot recalculates affected resources\n   └─> Pilot pushes incremental updates to proxies\n```\n\n### Key Architecture Packages\n\n#### Core Model & State (`pilot/pkg/model/`)\n- **`push_context.go`** - Aggregates all mesh state needed for configuration generation\n- **`service.go`** - Represents services in the mesh\n- **`proxy.go`** - Represents a proxy instance (Envoy sidecar or gateway)\n- **`config.go`** - Handles Istio configuration objects\n- **`xds_cache.go`** - Caches generated xDS resources per proxy\n\n#### xDS Implementation (`pilot/pkg/xds/`)\n- **`discovery.go`** - Core `DiscoveryServer` that implements xDS APIs\n- **`ads.go`** - Implements Aggregated Discovery Service (ADS) gRPC stream handling\n- **`generators/`** or individual files (`lds.go`, `rds.go`, `cds.go`, `eds.go`) - Generate config per resource type\n\n#### Service Registry (`pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/`)\n- Abstracts service discovery from different platforms (Kubernetes, Consul, etc.)\n- For Kubernetes: `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/`\n- Watches for service/endpoint changes and triggers Pilot pushes\n\n#### Networking & Configuration (`pilot/pkg/networking/`)\n- Converts high-level Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule, etc.) to Envoy configuration\n- Handles traffic management policies, security policies, telemetry\n\n#### Security (`security/pkg/`)\n- **`pki/`** - Certificate generation and management\n- **`server/`** - Certificate distribution server (implements SDS)\n- **`nodeagent/`** - Node-level security agent (in older versions)\n\n#### Common Utilities (`pkg/`)\n- **`pkg/kube/`** - Kubernetes client utilities\n- **`pkg/log/`** - Structured logging\n- **`pkg/xds/`** - Common xDS types and utilities\n- **`pkg/model/`** - Shared data models\n\n### Integration with Kubernetes\n\nIstio's control plane integrates deeply with Kubernetes:\n\n1. **Service Discovery:** Pilot watches Kubernetes Services and Endpoints\n2. **Configuration Injection:** Pilot operator injects Envoy sidecar proxies into Pods\n3. **CRD Management:** Pilot validates and stores Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule, etc.)\n4. **RBAC:** Uses Kubernetes RBAC for access control\n\n**Key Kubernetes integration points:**\n- `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/` - Main Kubernetes controller\n- `pilot/pkg/kube/` - Kubernetes client wrappers\n- Service and Endpoint watchers trigger push events\n- Pod injection handled by admission webhooks\n\n### Configuration Push Model\n\nPilot uses a **debouncing model** for pushing updates:\n\n1. **Event Detection:** Kubernetes event triggers a config change detector\n2. **Debouncing:** Multiple rapid changes are batched (configurable delay)\n3. **Push Decision:** Pilot determines which proxies need updates\n4. **Incremental Push:** Only changed resources are sent to proxies (via Delta xDS)\n\nThis prevents the \"thundering herd\" problem where all proxies would request updates simultaneously.\n\n---\n\n## First Contribution Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Set Up Your Development Environment\n\n1. **Fork the repository** on GitHub: https://github.com/istio/istio\n2. **Clone your fork:**\n   ```bash\n   git clone https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/istio.git\n   cd istio\n   ```\n3. **Add upstream remote:**\n   ```bash\n   git remote add upstream https://github.com/istio/istio.git\n   ```\n\n### Step 2: Find a Good First Issue\n\nVisit the Istio repository's issue tracker and look for issues labeled:\n- `good first issue` - Explicitly marked as beginner-friendly\n- `help wanted` - Community contributions welcome\n- `area/pilot` or `area/security` - Control plane specific issues\n\n**Issue search:** https://github.com/istio/istio/issues?q=label%3A%22good+first+issue%22\n\n### Step 3: Create a Feature Branch\n\n```bash\ngit fetch upstream\ngit checkout -b fix/your-feature-name upstream/main\n```\n\nUse clear branch naming:\n- `fix/bug-description` - Bug fixes\n- `feat/feature-name` - New features\n- `docs/update-description` - Documentation updates\n\n### Step 4: Make Your Changes\n\nMake your code changes following Istio conventions:\n\n**Key Principles:**\n- Make small, focused changes\n- One logical change per commit\n- Write clear commit messages\n\n**Example change workflow for a bug fix in Pilot:**\n\n1. Identify the component (e.g., `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go`)\n2. Read existing code and tests\n3. Make minimal changes\n4. Write/update tests\n5. Verify the build and tests pass\n\n### Step 5: Run Pre-commit Checks\n\nBefore committing, run the linters and formatters:\n\n```bash\nmake precommit\n```\n\nThis automatically:\n- Formats Go code (`gofmt`)\n- Formats Python code (if applicable)\n- Runs Go linters\n\n**Individual checks:**\n```bash\nmake format       # Format all code\nmake fmt          # Alias for format\nmake lint         # Run all linters\nmake lint-go      # Go-specific linting\n```\n\n### Step 6: Write and Run Tests\n\nIstio tests are organized by package. Find and run tests for your changes:\n\n```bash\n# Run all tests (warning: slow)\nmake test\n\n# Run tests for a specific package\ngo test ./pilot/pkg/xds/...\n\n# Run a specific test\ngo test ./pilot/pkg/xds -run TestADS -v\n\n# Run tests with race detection\ngo test -race ./pilot/pkg/xds/...\n```\n\n**Writing Tests:**\n- Place test files next to the code they test (e.g., `ads.go` and `ads_test.go`)\n- Use table-driven tests for multiple cases\n- Follow Go testing conventions\n- Example: `pilot/pkg/xds/ads_test.go`\n\n**Test Organization Example:**\n```\npilot/pkg/xds/\n├── ads.go         # Implementation\n├── ads_test.go    # Unit tests\n├── lds.go         # Implementation\n├── lds_test.go    # Unit tests\n└── ...\n```\n\n### Step 7: Commit Your Changes\n\nWrite clear commit messages:\n\n```bash\ngit add <modified-files>\ngit commit -m \"Fix: description of what was fixed\n\nThis describes why the fix was needed and what problem it solves.\nReference issue: Closes #1234\n\"\n```\n\n**Commit message guidelines:**\n- First line: Brief summary (under 70 characters)\n- Blank line\n- Detailed explanation\n- Reference GitHub issues if applicable\n- One logical change per commit\n\n### Step 8: Submit a Pull Request\n\n1. **Push your branch:**\n   ```bash\n   git push origin fix/your-feature-name\n   ```\n\n2. **Create PR on GitHub:**\n   - Go to https://github.com/istio/istio/compare\n   - Select your branch\n   - Fill in the PR template with:\n     - **What this PR does:** Clear description\n     - **Why:** Problem it solves\n     - **Checklist:** Mark completed items\n     - **Testing:** How you tested the changes\n\n3. **PR Requirements:**\n   - Must pass CI checks (GitHub Actions)\n   - Code review required (minimum 1-2 reviewers)\n   - Tests must pass\n   - Must pass linters and pre-commit checks\n   - Documentation updated if applicable\n\n### Step 9: Respond to Code Review\n\n1. **Wait for reviewers:** The Istio team will review your PR\n2. **Address feedback:** Make requested changes\n3. **Push updates:**\n   ```bash\n   git add <modified-files>\n   git commit -m \"Address review feedback: specific changes\"\n   git push origin fix/your-feature-name\n   ```\n4. **Discussion:** Engage respectfully in PR discussions\n\n### Step 10: Merge and Celebrate\n\nOnce approved, a maintainer will merge your PR. Your code is now part of Istio!\n\n---\n\n## Common Development Tasks\n\n### Building Docker Images\n\nAfter modifying code, build Docker images for testing:\n\n```bash\n# Build all images\nmake docker.build\n\n# Build specific images\nmake docker.build HUB=myregistry TAG=myversion\n\n# Push to registry\nmake docker.push HUB=myregistry TAG=myversion\n```\n\n### Debugging\n\n**Enable debug logging:**\n```bash\nmake build DEBUG=1\n```\n\n**View environment:**\n```bash\nmake show.env      # Show all environment variables\nmake show.goenv    # Show Go-specific variables\n```\n\n### Running Integration Tests\n\nFor more comprehensive testing:\n\n```bash\n# Run integration tests (requires Kubernetes)\nmake test-integration\n```\n\nSee `tests/integration/README.md` for detailed integration testing guidance.\n\n### Checking for Generated Code Changes\n\nSome files are generated (protobuf, CRDs, etc.):\n\n```bash\n# Update all generated code\nmake gen\n\n# Check if anything needs to be generated\nmake gen-check\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Deep Dive: Example Data Flow\n\nLet's trace a concrete example: **Creating a new VirtualService that routes traffic**\n\n### Step 1: Configuration is Created\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1\nkind: VirtualService\nmetadata:\n  name: my-service\nspec:\n  hosts:\n  - my-service\n  http:\n  - match:\n    - uri:\n        prefix: /api\n    route:\n    - destination:\n        host: my-service\n        port:\n          number: 8080\n```\n\n### Step 2: Pilot Detects the Change\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/`\n- Configuration is stored in Kubernetes etcd\n- Pilot watches for changes via Kubernetes API\n- Event triggers the configuration controller\n\n### Step 3: Push Request is Created\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go`\n- A `PushRequest` is created containing which proxies need updates\n- Push queue manages the order of pushes (`pilot/pkg/xds/pushqueue.go`)\n\n### Step 4: Configuration is Generated\n- **For each connected proxy:**\n  1. **Listener Config (LDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go`\n     - Creates HTTP listener on port 80/443\n     - Hooks in route config\n  2. **Route Config (RDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go`\n     - Generates routing rules matching `/api` prefix\n     - Points to `my-service` cluster\n  3. **Cluster Config (CDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go`\n     - Creates cluster entry for `my-service`\n  4. **Endpoint Config (EDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go`\n     - Lists actual pod IPs for `my-service`\n\n### Step 5: Pilot Pushes to Proxies\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go`\n- Connected proxies receive updates via gRPC stream\n- Updates are incremental (only changed resources)\n\n### Step 6: Proxies Apply Configuration\n- Each Envoy proxy receives the xDS push\n- Routes requests to `/api` to `my-service` endpoints\n- Load balancing happens at the proxy level\n\n**Key Takeaway:** The entire flow from Kubernetes resource to traffic routing happens through a series of watchers, generators, and xDS pushes.\n\n---\n\n## Resources and References\n\n### Official Documentation\n- **Istio Project:** https://istio.io\n- **Community:** https://github.com/istio/community\n- **Issue Tracker:** https://github.com/istio/istio/issues\n- **GitHub Discussions:** https://github.com/istio/istio/discussions\n\n### Related Repositories\n- **Istio APIs:** https://github.com/istio/api - API definitions and CRDs\n- **Common Build Files:** https://github.com/istio/common-files\n- **Community:** https://github.com/istio/community - Contribution guidelines\n\n### Useful Wiki Pages\n- **Development Conventions:** https://github.com/istio/istio/wiki/Development-Conventions\n- **Writing Fast and Lean Code:** https://github.com/istio/istio/wiki/Writing-Fast-and-Lean-Code\n- **Integration Tests Guide:** `tests/integration/README.md` in the repo\n\n### Key Source Files for Reference\n| Component | Key Files | Purpose |\n|-----------|-----------|---------|\n| **xDS Protocol** | `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go`, `discovery.go` | Main xDS server implementation |\n| **Configuration Generation** | `pilot/pkg/xds/{lds,rds,cds,eds}.go` | Per-type resource generators |\n| **Service Discovery** | `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/` | Kubernetes integration |\n| **Networking** | `pilot/pkg/networking/` | Traffic policy handling |\n| **Security** | `security/pkg/pki/`, `security/pkg/server/` | Certificate management |\n| **Model** | `pilot/pkg/model/` | Core data structures |\n\n---\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Build Issues\n\n**Error: \"VERSION not detected\"**\n- Solution: Ensure `VERSION` file exists in repo root: `cat VERSION`\n\n**Error: \"go-junit-report not found\"**\n- Solution: Install it manually: `go install github.com/jstemmer/go-junit-report@latest`\n- Or use container build: `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=1 make test`\n\n**Docker build fails with old Docker version**\n- Solution: Upgrade Docker to latest version\n\n### Test Failures\n\n**Tests timeout**\n- Try running a single test first: `go test ./pilot/pkg/xds -run TestADS`\n- Increase timeout: `go test -timeout 5m ./pilot/pkg/xds/...`\n\n**Race condition failures**\n- Run with race detector: `go test -race ./pilot/pkg/xds/...`\n- May indicate concurrent access bugs\n\n### Contributing Tips\n\n**Before opening a PR:**\n1. Run `make precommit` to catch linting issues\n2. Run `make test` for your package to verify tests pass\n3. Check that `make gen-check` passes (no generated code out of sync)\n4. Read the PR template carefully\n\n**Getting help:**\n- Ask in GitHub Discussions: https://github.com/istio/istio/discussions\n- Check existing issues and PRs for similar problems\n- Reach out to code reviewers with specific questions\n\n---\n\n## Next Steps\n\n1. **Clone the repository** and run `make build`\n2. **Pick a good first issue** from the GitHub issue tracker\n3. **Read the code** related to your issue\n4. **Write tests** for your fix\n5. **Submit a PR** following the workflow above\n6. **Iterate** based on review feedback\n\nWelcome to the Istio community! The control plane (istiod) is the core component that manages service discovery, configuration, and security in the Istio service mesh.\n\n---\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n1. [Build Prerequisites](#build-prerequisites)\n2. [Architecture Overview](#architecture-overview)\n3. [First Contribution Workflow](#first-contribution-workflow)\n\n---\n\n## Build Prerequisites\n\n### System Requirements\n\n**Go Version:** Go 1.24.0 or later\n\nThe project uses Go modules (`GO111MODULE=on`). You can verify your Go installation:\n\n```bash\ngo version\n```\n\n### Clone the Repository\n\nClone the repository with all submodules:\n\n```bash\ngit clone --recursive https://github.com/istio/istio.git\ncd istio\n```\n\nIf you've already cloned without submodules:\n\n```bash\ngit submodule update --init --recursive\n```\n\n### Build Options\n\nIstio supports two build modes:\n\n#### Option 1: Local Build (Recommended for Development)\n\nThis uses your local Go toolchain:\n\n```bash\nexport BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=0\nmake build\n```\n\n#### Option 2: Container Build\n\nUses Docker to build in a consistent environment (only requires `make` and `docker`):\n\n```bash\nexport BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=1\nmake build\n```\n\nIf you don't set `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER`, it defaults to `0` (local build).\n\n### Building Core Binaries\n\nThe main control plane components are built from the following targets:\n\n**Build all components:**\n```bash\nmake build\n```\n\n**Build specific binaries:**\n```bash\nmake pilot-discovery    # Main Pilot/istiod discovery server\nmake pilot-agent        # Sidecar proxy agent\nmake istioctl           # CLI tool for managing Istio\n```\n\nThese binaries are placed in `$(TARGET_OUT)` (default: `out/release/`).\n\n**Build for Linux containers:**\n```bash\nmake build-linux\n```\n\nThis is required before building Docker images and outputs to `out/linux_release/`.\n\n### Initialize Build Environment\n\nBefore your first build, initialize the build environment to download Envoy and other dependencies:\n\n```bash\nmake init\n```\n\nThis step:\n- Downloads the Envoy binary (matching the SHA defined in `pilot/docker/Dockerfile.base`)\n- Prepares dependencies declared in `istio.deps`\n\nThe initialization output is placed in `$(TARGET_OUT)/istio_is_init`.\n\n### Build Configuration\n\nKey environment variables and Makefile variables:\n\n| Variable | Purpose | Example |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER` | Use Docker for builds | `0` (local) or `1` (container) |\n| `TARGET_OUT` | Output directory for binaries | `out/release/` |\n| `ISTIO_BIN` | Directory for test binaries | `$(GOPATH)/bin` |\n| `DEBUG` | Enable debug symbols | `0` (default), `1` (enable) |\n| `GOPROXY` | Go module proxy | `https://proxy.golang.org` |\n\nExample: Build with debug symbols:\n```bash\nDEBUG=1 make build\n```\n\n### Verify Your Build\n\nAfter building, verify the binaries were created:\n\n```bash\nls -l out/release/pilot-discovery\nls -l out/release/pilot-agent\nls -l out/release/istioctl\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n### Control Plane Components\n\nThe Istio control plane (istiod) is a unified component that manages three primary functions:\n\n#### 1. **Pilot** - Configuration and Discovery\n- **Responsibility:** Manages service discovery and pushes dynamic configuration to Envoy proxies\n- **Key Packages:** `pilot/pkg/xds/`, `pilot/pkg/model/`, `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/`\n- **Entry Point:** `pilot/cmd/pilot-discovery/main.go` → `app.NewRootCommand()`\n\nPilot converts Kubernetes Service and Istio configuration objects into Envoy configuration. It serves as the xDS server that proxies connect to for runtime updates.\n\n**Example Data Flow:**\n1. Administrator creates a `VirtualService` resource in Kubernetes\n2. Pilot watches Kubernetes API and detects the change\n3. Pilot's configuration controller processes the `VirtualService`\n4. Pilot's model generates updated Envoy configuration (via `pilot/pkg/networking/`)\n5. Pilot pushes updates to connected proxies via xDS\n\n#### 2. **Citadel** - Certificate Management\n- **Responsibility:** Issues and rotates certificates for mTLS between services\n- **Key Packages:** `security/pkg/pki/`, `security/pkg/server/`\n\nCitadel manages the service mesh PKI (Public Key Infrastructure) by:\n- Issuing short-lived certificates to workloads\n- Managing certificate rotation\n- Implementing secret discovery service (SDS) for distributing certificates to proxies\n\n#### 3. **Galley** - Configuration Validation and Distribution (Now Integrated into Pilot)\n- **Responsibility:** Validates Istio configuration before it's applied\n- **Key Packages:** `pilot/pkg/config/`\n\nGalley validates configuration and ensures consistency across the mesh.\n\n### The xDS Protocol\n\nThe core communication mechanism between Pilot and Envoy proxies is **xDS** (extensible Discovery Service), a set of APIs defined by Envoy:\n\n| Protocol | Purpose | Package |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| **ADS** | Aggregated Discovery Service - main gRPC stream | `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go` |\n| **CDS** | Cluster Discovery Service - defines upstream clusters | `pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go` |\n| **RDS** | Route Discovery Service - defines routing rules | `pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go` |\n| **LDS** | Listener Discovery Service - defines listeners | `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go` |\n| **EDS** | Endpoint Discovery Service - defines service endpoints | `pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go` |\n| **SDS** | Secret Discovery Service - distributes certificates | `pilot/pkg/xds/sds.go` |\n| **ECDS** | Extension Config Discovery Service | `pilot/pkg/xds/ecds.go` |\n\n**xDS Data Flow Example:**\n\n```\n1. Envoy proxy connects to Pilot via gRPC (ADS)\n   └─> Connection handled by DiscoveryStream in pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go\n\n2. Proxy sends discovery request:\n   ├─> \"Give me my listeners\" (LDS)\n   ├─> \"Give me my routes\" (RDS)\n   ├─> \"Give me my clusters\" (CDS)\n   └─> \"Give me my endpoints\" (EDS)\n\n3. Pilot generates configuration:\n   ├─> LDS Generator creates listener config (pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go:119)\n   ├─> RDS Generator creates route config (pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go)\n   ├─> CDS Generator creates cluster config (pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go)\n   └─> EDS Generator creates endpoints (pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go)\n\n4. Pilot pushes response back to proxy\n   └─> Proxy applies configuration to its traffic handling\n\n5. When config changes (e.g., new Service):\n   ├─> Kubernetes event triggers Pilot's watchers\n   ├─> Pilot recalculates affected resources\n   └─> Pilot pushes incremental updates to proxies\n```\n\n### Key Architecture Packages\n\n#### Core Model & State (`pilot/pkg/model/`)\n- **`push_context.go`** - Aggregates all mesh state needed for configuration generation\n- **`service.go`** - Represents services in the mesh\n- **`proxy.go`** - Represents a proxy instance (Envoy sidecar or gateway)\n- **`config.go`** - Handles Istio configuration objects\n- **`xds_cache.go`** - Caches generated xDS resources per proxy\n\n#### xDS Implementation (`pilot/pkg/xds/`)\n- **`discovery.go`** - Core `DiscoveryServer` that implements xDS APIs\n- **`ads.go`** - Implements Aggregated Discovery Service (ADS) gRPC stream handling\n- **`generators/`** or individual files (`lds.go`, `rds.go`, `cds.go`, `eds.go`) - Generate config per resource type\n\n#### Service Registry (`pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/`)\n- Abstracts service discovery from different platforms (Kubernetes, Consul, etc.)\n- For Kubernetes: `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/`\n- Watches for service/endpoint changes and triggers Pilot pushes\n\n#### Networking & Configuration (`pilot/pkg/networking/`)\n- Converts high-level Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule, etc.) to Envoy configuration\n- Handles traffic management policies, security policies, telemetry\n\n#### Security (`security/pkg/`)\n- **`pki/`** - Certificate generation and management\n- **`server/`** - Certificate distribution server (implements SDS)\n- **`nodeagent/`** - Node-level security agent (in older versions)\n\n#### Common Utilities (`pkg/`)\n- **`pkg/kube/`** - Kubernetes client utilities\n- **`pkg/log/`** - Structured logging\n- **`pkg/xds/`** - Common xDS types and utilities\n- **`pkg/model/`** - Shared data models\n\n### Integration with Kubernetes\n\nIstio's control plane integrates deeply with Kubernetes:\n\n1. **Service Discovery:** Pilot watches Kubernetes Services and Endpoints\n2. **Configuration Injection:** Pilot operator injects Envoy sidecar proxies into Pods\n3. **CRD Management:** Pilot validates and stores Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule, etc.)\n4. **RBAC:** Uses Kubernetes RBAC for access control\n\n**Key Kubernetes integration points:**\n- `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/` - Main Kubernetes controller\n- `pilot/pkg/kube/` - Kubernetes client wrappers\n- Service and Endpoint watchers trigger push events\n- Pod injection handled by admission webhooks\n\n### Configuration Push Model\n\nPilot uses a **debouncing model** for pushing updates:\n\n1. **Event Detection:** Kubernetes event triggers a config change detector\n2. **Debouncing:** Multiple rapid changes are batched (configurable delay)\n3. **Push Decision:** Pilot determines which proxies need updates\n4. **Incremental Push:** Only changed resources are sent to proxies (via Delta xDS)\n\nThis prevents the \"thundering herd\" problem where all proxies would request updates simultaneously.\n\n---\n\n## First Contribution Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Set Up Your Development Environment\n\n1. **Fork the repository** on GitHub: https://github.com/istio/istio\n2. **Clone your fork:**\n   ```bash\n   git clone https://github.com/YOUR_USERNAME/istio.git\n   cd istio\n   ```\n3. **Add upstream remote:**\n   ```bash\n   git remote add upstream https://github.com/istio/istio.git\n   ```\n\n### Step 2: Find a Good First Issue\n\nVisit the Istio repository's issue tracker and look for issues labeled:\n- `good first issue` - Explicitly marked as beginner-friendly\n- `help wanted` - Community contributions welcome\n- `area/pilot` or `area/security` - Control plane specific issues\n\n**Issue search:** https://github.com/istio/istio/issues?q=label%3A%22good+first+issue%22\n\n### Step 3: Create a Feature Branch\n\n```bash\ngit fetch upstream\ngit checkout -b fix/your-feature-name upstream/main\n```\n\nUse clear branch naming:\n- `fix/bug-description` - Bug fixes\n- `feat/feature-name` - New features\n- `docs/update-description` - Documentation updates\n\n### Step 4: Make Your Changes\n\nMake your code changes following Istio conventions:\n\n**Key Principles:**\n- Make small, focused changes\n- One logical change per commit\n- Write clear commit messages\n\n**Example change workflow for a bug fix in Pilot:**\n\n1. Identify the component (e.g., `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go`)\n2. Read existing code and tests\n3. Make minimal changes\n4. Write/update tests\n5. Verify the build and tests pass\n\n### Step 5: Run Pre-commit Checks\n\nBefore committing, run the linters and formatters:\n\n```bash\nmake precommit\n```\n\nThis automatically:\n- Formats Go code (`gofmt`)\n- Formats Python code (if applicable)\n- Runs Go linters\n\n**Individual checks:**\n```bash\nmake format       # Format all code\nmake fmt          # Alias for format\nmake lint         # Run all linters\nmake lint-go      # Go-specific linting\n```\n\n### Step 6: Write and Run Tests\n\nIstio tests are organized by package. Find and run tests for your changes:\n\n```bash\n# Run all tests (warning: slow)\nmake test\n\n# Run tests for a specific package\ngo test ./pilot/pkg/xds/...\n\n# Run a specific test\ngo test ./pilot/pkg/xds -run TestADS -v\n\n# Run tests with race detection\ngo test -race ./pilot/pkg/xds/...\n```\n\n**Writing Tests:**\n- Place test files next to the code they test (e.g., `ads.go` and `ads_test.go`)\n- Use table-driven tests for multiple cases\n- Follow Go testing conventions\n- Example: `pilot/pkg/xds/ads_test.go`\n\n**Test Organization Example:**\n```\npilot/pkg/xds/\n├── ads.go         # Implementation\n├── ads_test.go    # Unit tests\n├── lds.go         # Implementation\n├── lds_test.go    # Unit tests\n└── ...\n```\n\n### Step 7: Commit Your Changes\n\nWrite clear commit messages:\n\n```bash\ngit add <modified-files>\ngit commit -m \"Fix: description of what was fixed\n\nThis describes why the fix was needed and what problem it solves.\nReference issue: Closes #1234\n\"\n```\n\n**Commit message guidelines:**\n- First line: Brief summary (under 70 characters)\n- Blank line\n- Detailed explanation\n- Reference GitHub issues if applicable\n- One logical change per commit\n\n### Step 8: Submit a Pull Request\n\n1. **Push your branch:**\n   ```bash\n   git push origin fix/your-feature-name\n   ```\n\n2. **Create PR on GitHub:**\n   - Go to https://github.com/istio/istio/compare\n   - Select your branch\n   - Fill in the PR template with:\n     - **What this PR does:** Clear description\n     - **Why:** Problem it solves\n     - **Checklist:** Mark completed items\n     - **Testing:** How you tested the changes\n\n3. **PR Requirements:**\n   - Must pass CI checks (GitHub Actions)\n   - Code review required (minimum 1-2 reviewers)\n   - Tests must pass\n   - Must pass linters and pre-commit checks\n   - Documentation updated if applicable\n\n### Step 9: Respond to Code Review\n\n1. **Wait for reviewers:** The Istio team will review your PR\n2. **Address feedback:** Make requested changes\n3. **Push updates:**\n   ```bash\n   git add <modified-files>\n   git commit -m \"Address review feedback: specific changes\"\n   git push origin fix/your-feature-name\n   ```\n4. **Discussion:** Engage respectfully in PR discussions\n\n### Step 10: Merge and Celebrate\n\nOnce approved, a maintainer will merge your PR. Your code is now part of Istio!\n\n---\n\n## Common Development Tasks\n\n### Building Docker Images\n\nAfter modifying code, build Docker images for testing:\n\n```bash\n# Build all images\nmake docker.build\n\n# Build specific images\nmake docker.build HUB=myregistry TAG=myversion\n\n# Push to registry\nmake docker.push HUB=myregistry TAG=myversion\n```\n\n### Debugging\n\n**Enable debug logging:**\n```bash\nmake build DEBUG=1\n```\n\n**View environment:**\n```bash\nmake show.env      # Show all environment variables\nmake show.goenv    # Show Go-specific variables\n```\n\n### Running Integration Tests\n\nFor more comprehensive testing:\n\n```bash\n# Run integration tests (requires Kubernetes)\nmake test-integration\n```\n\nSee `tests/integration/README.md` for detailed integration testing guidance.\n\n### Checking for Generated Code Changes\n\nSome files are generated (protobuf, CRDs, etc.):\n\n```bash\n# Update all generated code\nmake gen\n\n# Check if anything needs to be generated\nmake gen-check\n```\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Deep Dive: Example Data Flow\n\nLet's trace a concrete example: **Creating a new VirtualService that routes traffic**\n\n### Step 1: Configuration is Created\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1\nkind: VirtualService\nmetadata:\n  name: my-service\nspec:\n  hosts:\n  - my-service\n  http:\n  - match:\n    - uri:\n        prefix: /api\n    route:\n    - destination:\n        host: my-service\n        port:\n          number: 8080\n```\n\n### Step 2: Pilot Detects the Change\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/`\n- Configuration is stored in Kubernetes etcd\n- Pilot watches for changes via Kubernetes API\n- Event triggers the configuration controller\n\n### Step 3: Push Request is Created\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go`\n- A `PushRequest` is created containing which proxies need updates\n- Push queue manages the order of pushes (`pilot/pkg/xds/pushqueue.go`)\n\n### Step 4: Configuration is Generated\n- **For each connected proxy:**\n  1. **Listener Config (LDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/lds.go`\n     - Creates HTTP listener on port 80/443\n     - Hooks in route config\n  2. **Route Config (RDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/rds.go`\n     - Generates routing rules matching `/api` prefix\n     - Points to `my-service` cluster\n  3. **Cluster Config (CDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/cds.go`\n     - Creates cluster entry for `my-service`\n  4. **Endpoint Config (EDS):** `pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go`\n     - Lists actual pod IPs for `my-service`\n\n### Step 5: Pilot Pushes to Proxies\n- **Component:** `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go`\n- Connected proxies receive updates via gRPC stream\n- Updates are incremental (only changed resources)\n\n### Step 6: Proxies Apply Configuration\n- Each Envoy proxy receives the xDS push\n- Routes requests to `/api` to `my-service` endpoints\n- Load balancing happens at the proxy level\n\n**Key Takeaway:** The entire flow from Kubernetes resource to traffic routing happens through a series of watchers, generators, and xDS pushes.\n\n---\n\n## Resources and References\n\n### Official Documentation\n- **Istio Project:** https://istio.io\n- **Community:** https://github.com/istio/community\n- **Issue Tracker:** https://github.com/istio/istio/issues\n- **GitHub Discussions:** https://github.com/istio/istio/discussions\n\n### Related Repositories\n- **Istio APIs:** https://github.com/istio/api - API definitions and CRDs\n- **Common Build Files:** https://github.com/istio/common-files\n- **Community:** https://github.com/istio/community - Contribution guidelines\n\n### Useful Wiki Pages\n- **Development Conventions:** https://github.com/istio/istio/wiki/Development-Conventions\n- **Writing Fast and Lean Code:** https://github.com/istio/istio/wiki/Writing-Fast-and-Lean-Code\n- **Integration Tests Guide:** `tests/integration/README.md` in the repo\n\n### Key Source Files for Reference\n| Component | Key Files | Purpose |\n|-----------|-----------|---------|\n| **xDS Protocol** | `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go`, `discovery.go` | Main xDS server implementation |\n| **Configuration Generation** | `pilot/pkg/xds/{lds,rds,cds,eds}.go` | Per-type resource generators |\n| **Service Discovery** | `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/` | Kubernetes integration |\n| **Networking** | `pilot/pkg/networking/` | Traffic policy handling |\n| **Security** | `security/pkg/pki/`, `security/pkg/server/` | Certificate management |\n| **Model** | `pilot/pkg/model/` | Core data structures |\n\n---\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Build Issues\n\n**Error: \"VERSION not detected\"**\n- Solution: Ensure `VERSION` file exists in repo root: `cat VERSION`\n\n**Error: \"go-junit-report not found\"**\n- Solution: Install it manually: `go install github.com/jstemmer/go-junit-report@latest`\n- Or use container build: `BUILD_WITH_CONTAINER=1 make test`\n\n**Docker build fails with old Docker version**\n- Solution: Upgrade Docker to latest version\n\n### Test Failures\n\n**Tests timeout**\n- Try running a single test first: `go test ./pilot/pkg/xds -run TestADS`\n- Increase timeout: `go test -timeout 5m ./pilot/pkg/xds/...`\n\n**Race condition failures**\n- Run with race detector: `go test -race ./pilot/pkg/xds/...`\n- May indicate concurrent access bugs\n\n### Contributing Tips\n\n**Before opening a PR:**\n1. Run `make precommit` to catch linting issues\n2. Run `make test` for your package to verify tests pass\n3. Check that `make gen-check` passes (no generated code out of sync)\n4. Read the PR template carefully\n\n**Getting help:**\n- Ask in GitHub Discussions: https://github.com/istio/istio/discussions\n- Check existing issues and PRs for similar problems\n- Reach out to code reviewers with specific questions\n\n---\n\n## Next Steps\n\n1. **Clone the repository** and run `make build`\n2. **Pick a good first issue** from the GitHub issue tracker\n3. **Read the code** related to your issue\n4. **Write tests** for your fix\n5. **Submit a PR** following the workflow above\n6. **Iterate** based on review feedback\n\nWelcome to the Istio community! Happy coding!\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
